DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
- Memory evolution, form coverage: the input gives the evolution forms sampled for
this episode (each with a natural-language description and a micro-example). **
Each form must occur naturally at least once**, riding the plot rather than
being mechanically aligned; the examples only illustrate the form itself, and
their topics, proper nouns and numbers must never be copied.
- Memory evolution, distribution over time: evolutions must be spread across
different sessions. Establish the old fact naturally in an earlier session,
then let it evolve a stretch of time later. The fact marked as a "two-hop chain
" must go through two evolutions, A to B to C (establish, evolve, evolve again)
, so that later question generation has a distractor closer to the current
value to choose from. Do not evolve too early, and leave enough back-and-forth
between two adjacent evolutions.
- Memory evolution, plot and boundaries: evolution plots are a hard requirement for
this episode, and the episode must not consist of additions only with nothing
changing. But not every session needs an evolution; most sessions being nothing
but new information or pure chat is normal.
- Memory evolution, it must be matchable: the lines where an evolution happens must
let the reader see the old-to-new correspondence at a glance. The user
naturally states what it became, which earlier thing it concerns, why, and
whether there is a deadline or an exception ("that xx from before, turns out
..."). It must not be waved through with a single "that changed", or later
stages cannot tell old from new.
- Memory evolution, do not bed down profile along the way: beyond what the
evolution forms require, **as a rule bury no further new stable profile facts**.
An evolution builds on the old fact and completes only the expression the
evolution needs; do not drag in a ring of surrounding profile such as job,
family or habits along with it. Material that would otherwise become profile
must be rewritten as a disposable chitchat-style reaction (about right now only
, never landing as a long-term fact), and all the space saved goes to agent-
type plots and chitchat. **Exception**: if the sampled evolution is an event-
type one (retracting or correcting something that was going to happen), that
event must first be laid out naturally as a live thread the user is arranging
and keeping in mind (a reason, anticipation, the agent helping coordinate)
before it is retracted or corrected. That is required by the evolution and does
not count as surplus profile; such build-up stays about the event itself and
still drags in no surrounding stable profile such as job or family.
== user-type memory material ==
- **The user's lines must supply material for the agent side (hard requirement)**:
generating agent_method and agent_knowledge needs the user to bring back the
results of the last approach on their own ("did it your way, it worked", "it
blew up, got stuck at step two") and to say whether it went well, and then,
once a stretch of work wraps up or the same snag is hit repeatedly, to casually
ask for it to be distilled ("turn what you just did into steps i can follow
from now on", "pull one lesson out of these last few times so we do not do this
again"). Vary the hooks and grow them out of the plot; do not run the same
phrasing every episode, and do not force one just to make up a count.
- User profile (user_profile) material: have the user scatter **a bare minimum of**
long-term facts around the given topic (identity, preferences, relationships,
habits, boundaries and so on) naturally across the whole chat, limited to what
the sampled evolution forms need and no further. Do not have every session
piling on the user's own profile. The key point is: **let some of those facts
evolve naturally over time**.
- Entity fact (fact) material: beyond the user's own profile, the chat may **
naturally surface a small number of facts about standalone entities themselves
**: the names, models, prices, quantities, opening or schedule times, current
states or stable attributes of specific objects, shops, places, devices,
transit lines, institutions or works (for example "the shop downstairs is
closed on Mondays", "the living-room AC is a three-year-old model", "the last
Line 2 train is 22:30", "that blood-pressure monitor is an Omron"). The test is
whether the core lands on **the entity itself and can be located independently
**, rather than on the user's preferences or relationships. Let these facts sit
25


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
scattered through the venting and the storytelling like lived-in detail, never
listed out as a spec card. A few scattered spots are enough and they need not
come in a block; when in doubt, give the screen time to agent_method and
agent_knowledge plots instead.
== agent-type memory material (needs enough supporting plot) ==
- **Agent-side proportion and plot density (hard constraint)**: beyond the user-
side facts, the episode must also **deliberately stage plots that can be
extracted as agent-type memories**, with all three types appearing naturally
where possible (one that genuinely does not fit the plot may be missing, but
the episode as a whole must carry a decent number). **Only the relative
proportion among the three types is fixed here**: agent_identity carries the
heaviest share (use both shapes, setting rules and assigning a role),
agent_method next, agent_knowledge least, that is agent_identity > agent_method
> agent_knowledge. How much weight the agent-side plots carry relative to the
user-side ones is decided by whether this episode leans user_centric or
agent_centric (see the corresponding requirement in this episode's task); no
overall share is pinned down here. Several rules spread across different
sessions, each with its own trigger, never bunched into one spot.
- **Agent behavior red line (hard constraint)**: the agent must never narrate any
memory-management action, and must never say "let me note that down", "I'll
change that one for you", "let me merge these", "I won't record this one". It
is simply chatting like a normal person and naturally remembering.
- **The agent must not spend the whole episode as a question machine and an echo
chamber (hard constraint)**: chains of "so what are you going to do" counter-
questions plus "got it", "okay", "noted" acknowledgments are the worst shape,
and agent memory cannot grow out of them. The agent needs screen time where it
contributes on its own initiative: showing its own approach as the task comes,
comparing options unprompted, saying its observations out loud, while keeping a
natural voice and never turning into a lecture just to contribute.
- **Agent-type memory must be extracted from existing plot (hard constraint)**:
first a task or a problem, then attempts and feedback, and only then a rule, a
method or a lesson. Never have a character recite definitions or read out a
charter just to hit a type, and never break the human feel for it.
- Definitions and tests for the three agent types (**the content must be
unambiguously long-term. The common waste is writing agent moments as a this-
round request, a one-off tone ask, advice about the current problem, or the
agent merely restating or confirming a rule the user just set: all of those
count only as episodic.**)
`agent_identity` (test): amid a real need, the user explicitly says how they want
the agent to collaborate by default from now on, or which boundaries must hold,
usually raised right after being bothered by some reply style. Both shapes
must be used: one is setting a rule, the other is **handing a class of tasks to
the agent and assigning it a role and a way of working** ("this kind of thing
is yours from now on", "from now on you are the one checking my books, run the
numbers first every time"). The test has three parts: the wording is explicitly
long-term ("from now on", "every time", "by default", "unless I say otherwise
", "stop"); it targets a recurring class of situations rather than only this
one reply; and the anchor words stay in the user's own line. The agent's
confirming echo does not become a memory of its own.
`agent_identity` (how to write it): the whole line must be colloquial, carry
emotion, and set exactly one point at a time. Write something like "ugh, from
now on can you just lead with the answer? i really don't have the patience to
scroll to the bottom for it", not requirements-document prose like "From now on
, when answering me, state the conclusion first, then list the key evidence.
Where content is uncertain you must say so explicitly and never guess". Do not
bundle two or three requirements into one message either: a real person raises
one point at a time, so if there are several rules they arrive in several goes,
separated by a few turns or a few sessions, each with its own trigger. And
never have the user read out an assistant charter for no reason.
`agent_method`: while actually helping the user with some class of task, the agent
naturally adopts and spells out a reusable procedure, checking order or
26


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
verification approach. It must be how the agent itself will act from now on,
not a lesson to the user about how to live. There must be a **cross-session**
evidence chain, and either opening works: one, the agent starts it, and as the
task lands it spells out "for this kind of thing I'll do X before Y from now on
" (wording like "I'll", "every time you send me", "from here on"); two, it just
does the thing that way without announcing it, the user brings back results a
session later, and the agent then spells out "from now on I'll handle this kind
of task X to Y to Z". Either way it must wait for the user to bring back
results and then be confirmed or corrected into shape by the agent before it
counts as surviving. A single set of steps this round, or steps handed to the
user to carry out, does not count.
`agent_knowledge`: only after cross-session result feedback, failure post-mortems,
comparison of several options or repeated verification may the agent naturally
state one stable lesson or judgment rule, and it must visibly carry its
provenance ("across these last few times I've found...", "having compared a few
approaches..."). It usually lands from the third session onward, once two or
three similar events have piled up; summarizing a rule on first encounter will
be rejected. It is a stable rule the agent banks for itself, not a one-line
takeaway about the current case, and common sense, a one-off suggestion or
empty self-praise must not be dressed up as knowledge.
---- (5) Output contract ----
== **Output format (hard constraint)** ==
Output exactly one JSON object, with no explanation and no code fences:
{"turns": [{"role": "user", "text": "hey, got steamrolled by a coworker again today
."}, {"role": "agent", "text": "What happened? Who was it this time?"}, ...]}
turns is **one flat, strictly alternating** conversation, user and agent one
message each in turn; do not split it into sessions yet (a later program cuts
it). role may only be user or agent. Write everything in natural, idiomatic
English. Make it full-length, mixing short and long messages; do not rush the
ending.
Memory Capture Prompt
You are annotating memory capture (extraction) for a long-term-memory benchmark.
The input is a multi-day, multi-session chat log between a real user and an AI
assistant - physically truncated at the target session (no later sessions exist
in the input) - plus a JSON fill-in skeleton for that **target session** (one
entry per turn).
Your task: extract memories **independently for every turn** of the target session,
filling each entry's memories list (0 to many per turn).
== Extraction scope (blind full extraction, per turn) ==
- Each memory may only be supported by its own turn; the rest of the dialogue is
only for understanding, reference resolution and time conversion (relative time
MUST be converted to an absolute date whenever the session date / turn time
make it convertible) - never credit other turns' information to this turn, and
never use later turns (no backfilling of later confirmations);
- Split a turn with multiple independent information points into multiple memories;
- **One memory = one atomic fact (long-term five types only)**: a single
memory_content must not cram two independently-standing facts joined by "and /
both ... and / as well as" (e.g. "the user has a son and uses a home monitor");
split them into separate memories even when they share the same type and
subject; but **episodic is recorded as a complete event unit** - multiple
actions/outcomes/feedback of the SAME event go into ONE memory and are exempt
from this split rule; only genuinely distinct events become separate memories;
- No cross-session dedup: even if the same information appeared earlier, or this
turn updates old information, extract what this turn itself expresses (later
stages handle consolidation);
- **High recall without duplication**: extract whenever a turn carries a concrete
object, state, request, question, suggestion, decision, evaluation,
27


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
confirmation, change or result; short, casual, joking or banter lines are not
worthless;
- **Incidental stable profile facts still count**: family or interpersonal
relationships dropped in passing ("my son"), items or tools the user owns or
uses long-term ("my home monitor"), and recurring personal habits or ways of
phrasing must still be extracted as user_profile even when they are a
background aside rather than the turn's main point - never skip them just
because they slipped out casually;
- Extract suitable long-term types first, then check for uncovered event
information; information fully covered by the other five types must not be re-
output as episodic with a mere 'the user said' shell;
- Turns with no extraction value get an empty list: no restatable semantics at all
(bare laughter, pure interjections, a lone emoji), or purely social lines
adding no new object, state, decision, request, condition or outcome (greetings
, thanks, courtesy, bare agreement, pure emotional reactions). Never blank a
contentful turn for convenience, and never invent memories for genuinely empty
ones.
== Role-type hard constraints ==
- **user** turns may only produce: episodic, user_profile, fact; never
agent_identity,agent_method or agent_knowledge;
- **assistant** turns may only produce: episodic, agent_identity,agent_method,
agent_knowledge; never user_profile, fact;
- If an information point belongs to a forbidden type by the rules: extract it as
episodic when it fits the episodic definition, otherwise drop it. Never force
it into an allowed type.
## Memory types
There are exactly 6 memory types:
- `episodic`: a concrete interaction or event, answering "what happened"
- `user_profile`: the user's own attributes, relationships, preferences, habits,
goals, constraints and conditional rules, answering "what is the user like /
what preferences and constraints do they have about themselves"
- `fact`: third-party-verifiable objective information - attributes, states, long-
term rules or completed concrete events of an independent entity or the
external world, answering "what is the state of that object / the external
world". **Its evidence root must be the user's statement** (see the fact
section)
- `agent_identity`: what the user explicitly requires about how the agent
collaborates, outputs and positions itself long-term, answering "how must/mustn
't the agent collaborate"
- `agent_method`: reusable generic steps, workflows or checklists the agent can
execute in similar tasks, answering "what steps should the agent follow"
- `agent_knowledge`: cross-task-reusable agent-side insight, method principles or
judgment frameworks, answering "what judgment should the agent apply"
## Memory-type classification rules
### Classification rules
| Type | Records | Cue |
|------|---------|-----|
| `episodic` | a concrete interaction event this turn | a one-off action: told /
requested / decided / tried / feedback / result |
| `user_profile` | the user's own attributes, relationships, preferences, habits,
goals, constraints, conditional rules | subject is the user, or describes the
user's preference/constraint |
| `fact` | third-party-verifiable **stable** objective info: entity attributes,
long-term rules, completed concrete events | subject is an independent entity
or the external world, and it still holds a week later |
| `agent_identity` | what the user requires about the agent's **long-term**
28


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
collaboration, output, positioning | the user tells the agent "from now on you
should/shouldn't..." |
| `agent_method` | generic steps/workflows/checklists the user teaches the agent |
the user says "for this kind of problem, first... then... finally..." |
| `agent_knowledge` | cross-task-reusable agent-side insight, method principles or
judgment frameworks | "this class of problems is usually caused by X", with
clear dialogue grounding, not a one-task conclusion |
Only `fact` is bound by the "a fact must originate from the user" hard constraint.
`agent_knowledge` records agent-side knowledge by nature and may rest on real
assistant statements as core evidence; but never relabel a one-task answer,
code artifact, search result, price or UI state, or an unsupported guess, as `
agent_knowledge` - those belong only in `episodic`.
### Notes
`user_profile` includes the user's conditional rules. The user's conditions/
constraints/rules about **themselves**, even phrased "if X then Y", are `
user_profile`:
- "if I've had insomnia, don't schedule high-intensity training" -> **user_profile
** (the user's constraint preference)
- "savings rate is usually 30%, dropping to 20-25% in big-spend months" -> **
user_profile** (the user's flexible goal)
- "no coffee after 2pm" -> **user_profile** (the user's habit constraint)
`agent_method` is only for a **generic way of working** taught to the agent,
subject = the agent, not the user:
- "for this kind of problem, check the logs first, then verify the config" ->
agent_method
- capture role mask: a workflow the user mandates directly is recorded as `
agent_identity`; only a reusable workflow an assistant turn itself forms/
validates is extracted as `agent_method`.
### High-frequency misclassification fixes
- "from now on default to answering me in Chinese" -> `agent_identity` (a long-term
requirement on agent behavior)
- "I prefer conclusions first, then the details" -> `user_profile` (the user's
preference)
- "the project is deployed on Kubernetes" -> `fact` (stable objective info the user
stated)
- "the cheapest Beijing--Shanghai flight is 680" -> **not a long-term memory** (a
volatile external state the assistant looked up; extract only an `episodic` if
there is a concrete interaction)
## Extraction discipline
- Do not mis-extract, cross-attribute, or dredge up historical memories
- History assists but never triggers: if a memory cannot stand on the target turn
alone after deleting all other turns, do not output it
- When the user merely asks about, follows up on or double-checks a historical item
("do you remember...", "what did I say last time") without restating or
confirming it in the target turn, extract only the asking/confirming event
itself; never unfold the historical item into a memory
- Generic acknowledgements from the assistant ("noted / got it / OK / understood")
are not valid result information; never write them as "completed" or as
standalone memories
## Causal boundary: no memory time travel
- When extracting for `Sx-Ty`, use only information directly expressed by that
target turn; earlier turns may only help resolve references and ellipses ("it /
that one / yes / still hurts")
29


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
- **Nothing from any later turn, later session, subsequent result or subsequent
confirmation may be backfilled into an earlier turn**
- Even if later text proves a guess right, do not rewrite the earlier guess as an
already-confirmed fact
- Names, quantities, causes, results or plan-execution status supplied later must
be attributed to the later turn itself, never written into earlier memories
- Example: T1 says "might be a battery issue" and T5 confirms "official diagnosis:
battery fault"; T1 may only record the suspicion, never "battery fault
confirmed"
## One information point, one home
- Never mechanically copy the same semantic fact into multiple long-term types just
to extract more
- **Long-term types take precedence for the same information point**: once
information is fully captured as `user_profile` / `fact` / `agent_identity` / `
agent_method` / `agent_knowledge`, do not re-output the same semantics as `
episodic`
- `episodic` records only concrete event information in the turn not already
covered by the other five types (an executed action, this round's outcome,
extra feedback); it must not be a long-term memory wrapped in a "the user said
..." shell
- The same turn may yield both a long-term memory and an episodic, but they must
cover different information points. "I'll never eat cilantro again, and I
returned today's bowl" yields a long-term dislike of cilantro + the return
event; not another "the user said they don't eat cilantro"
- Split two independent **long-term** information points in one turn into separate
memories; never extract the same point twice just because it is rephrased. But
**episodic is recorded as a complete event unit**: multiple actions, outcomes
and feedback of the SAME event go into ONE memory, not fragmented sentence by
sentence; only genuinely distinct events become separate memories
- If a turn reports an object's current state ("my shoe is broken"), the core is
the object's dynamic attribute: extract a `fact`, not a hollow "the user
described the shoe situation"
- If a turn describes an actual action ("I sent the shoe for repair"), the core is
a concrete event: extract an `episodic`
## Long-term memory admission
Except for `episodic`, every candidate must pass all four gates:
1. **Explicitness**: directly stated or explicitly confirmed by the target turn,
not assistant guesses, mood-matching or weak inference
2. **Correct subject**: the subject matches the type definition; no sneaky subject
swaps via paraphrase
3. **Future value**: recalling it across sessions would change answers, decisions
or execution
4. **Irreplaceability**: hard to rebuild cheaply from public knowledge, live tools,
docs or systems
These signals alone never prove long-term stability: the word "like", present tense
, a single repetition, assistant paraphrase, one success/failure, one purchase/
order/complaint.
## Not long-term memory
By default, do not admit:
- shared knowledge, common sense, basic concepts, term definitions
- information with an authoritative external source, or cheaply retrievable from
docs / code / config / CMDB / consoles / RAG / search / live tools
- parameters or format requests only meaningful this round, one-off emotions/
complaints, weakly supported inferences
- public entrances / links / dashboard addresses, rapidly changing states
- the assistant's speculation, mood-matching, gap-filling or unsupported summaries
30


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
## Literalness: jokes, hyperbole, sarcasm and metaphor are not taken literally
- Obvious jokes, venting, sarcasm, hyperbole, metaphor or figures of speech must
NOT have
their literal content extracted as `fact` / `user_profile` or any long-term
memory. E.g.
"my son is such a pig", "my boss is a vampire", "this junk laptop should be
smashed" must
not yield facts like "the user's son is a pig" / "the user's boss is a vampire",
nor a stable
attribute/profile of the user or any entity.
- Judge by conversational context and common sense: wording that is literally false
or clearly
emotional hyperbole is read as emotion/evaluation first, never as literal fact.
Absent a clear
long-term intent, prefer treating it as an emotional line over pinning it down
literally.
- Extract only the real meaning when such wording genuinely reveals a stable
attitude, evaluation
or relationship, defaulting to `episodic` (e.g. "the user is frustrated with
their son's recent
behavior"); promote to long-term memory only if the user separately makes an
explicit, reusably
long-term statement.
## Explicit user statements
- The user's explicit statements, requests and confirmations are strong long-term
candidates; no need to wait for repetition
- Typical forms: self-description ("my name is..." / "I like..." / "I usually..." /
"I value... more"), explicit requirements for the agent ("from now on you
default to..." / "always do X before Y" / "never..."), direct statements about
private facts ("I have a cat" / "the cat's name is...")
- "Explicit" only makes something a candidate; still check type attribution,
admission gates and boundaries
- The assistant's paraphrase, summary, guess or mood-matching never counts as an
explicit user statement unless the user clearly confirms it
- `agent_knowledge` is neither a "user-told" memory nor public knowledge: it must
be stable experiential insight the agent formed through task practice, long-
term retrospectives, multi-source comparison, research compression or explicit
lesson-drawing
## Type definitions
### 1. episodic
A concrete interaction or event this turn: questions, requests, notifications,
plans, decisions, attempts, suggestions, feedback, state changes, results.
- Extract by core event (not per sentence): one event goes into ONE complete `
episodic`; state its subject, action and necessary objects
- **Relative time MUST be resolved**: an episodic records an event and therefore
most often carries relative time. Whenever "yesterday / the day before
yesterday / last Friday evening / three days ago / last month / last year"
appears, it MUST be converted to an absolute date using the timestamps carried
in the dialogue context - the date on the owning session's header (`[Session N|
YYYY-MM-DD]`) together with the turn's own clock time (`[SN-TM|HH:MM]`).
Annotate the result in parentheses ("yesterday (2026-06-08)", "last Friday
evening (2026-06-05 evening)") or write the absolute date directly; only
anchorless, unlocatable vague wording ("recently", "a while back") may stay as-
is. Never invent a date and never miscompute one
- Lines with no restatable semantics (bare laughter, pure interjections, a lone
emoji, pure greetings/thanks/agreement, or a continuation that merely restates
prior content) may be left empty
- The assistant's advice for the user's current problem (even with clear steps/
31


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
checklists), executor = the user, is only `episodic`, never `agent_method` / `
agent_knowledge`
### 2. fact
Third-party-verifiable **stable** objective information: attributes, long-term
rules or completed concrete events of an independent entity or the external
world; subject is the independent entity, not the user or the agent.
- **Its evidence root must be the user's statement** (see "a fact must originate
from the user" above): assistant output, tool/retrieval returns and GUI
observations may not be a `fact` source
- Cue: does this still hold a week later? If it varies at will (price, weather,
balance, a single measurement, the transient state of an in-flight task) it is
not a `fact` - extract only an `episodic`
- The user's long-term relationship / ownership / responsibility toward an entity
belongs in `user_profile`
### 3. user_profile
The user's own attributes, relationships, preferences, habits, goals, constraints
and conditional rules, or the user's long-term stable relationship / ownership
/ responsibility toward an entity.
- The user's conditional rules about themselves ("if X then Y") also belong here
- One action is not a habit, one emotion is not a personality, one plan is not a
long-term goal
- Stage states are extractable but must keep their explicit sustained-intent signal
("preparing for an exam", "cutting sugar")
### 4. agent_identity
What the user requires about how the agent **long-term** collaborates, outputs,
positions itself, is constrained and responds. "from now on you...", "you may
not...", "by default you..."
- The subject must be the assistant itself; one-off format requests do not count
- If the user expresses a personal preference without asking the agent to act on it
long-term, it is `user_profile`
### 5. agent_method
Reusable generic steps, workflows or checklists, with the agent as subject. "for
this kind of problem, first... then... finally..."
- The executor must be the agent; advice for the user's current problem (even with
steps) is only `episodic`, not `agent_method`
- capture role mask: a workflow the user mandates directly goes to `agent_identity
`; only a reusable workflow an assistant turn itself forms/validates is
extracted as `agent_method`
### 6. agent_knowledge
Cross-task-reusable agent-side insight, method principles or judgment frameworks. "
this class of problems is usually caused by X"
- It records agent-side knowledge and may rest on real assistant statements as core
evidence (not bound by "must originate from the user")
- Never relabel a one-task answer, code artifact, search result, price or UI state,
or an unsupported guess, as `agent_knowledge` - those belong only in `episodic
`
- Exclude public knowledge; require clear dialogue grounding, not a one-task
conclusion
- `agent_method` and `agent_knowledge` may co-exist: transferable steps/workflows/
checklists -> `agent_method`; judgment rules or failure attributions formed
through retrospectives/comparison/verification -> `agent_knowledge`
## Quick decision table
- "What concrete action/advice/feedback happened?" -> `episodic`
- "What is the user like long-term/stage-wise, or stably related to whom/what?" ->
`user_profile`
32


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
- "What is the state of a private object outside the user?" -> `fact`
- "What did the user explicitly require the agent to always/never do?" -> `
agent_identity`
- "What steps can the agent follow in similar tasks?" -> `agent_method`
- "What stable judgment rule did the agent form from retrospectives/comparison/
repeated verification?" -> `agent_knowledge`
- If none of the six can be answered without guessing -> do not extract
## Memory boundaries
- Never extract the assistant's speculation, mood-matching or gap-filling
- Never extract meta-talk only meaningful to the current generation ("let me
summarize", "I can continue")
- Never treat public knowledge, standard medical/exercise common sense or product
manuals as the agent's private long-term memory
- Never treat premises implied by a question as confirmed facts; "is my shoe broken
?" never yields "the shoe is broken"
## memory_text requirements
`memory_text` is each memory's main description. It must be:
- faithful to the dialogue
- as concise as possible without losing key information
- normalized where necessary: resolve references and unify entities; never write "
this / that / he / it / what I said last time" and other expressions with no
independent referent - always complete them to entities or events already
explicit in the dialogue
- **references MUST be resolved to be independently understandable, context-free**:
for person and entity references keep the original wording and add the
resolved referent in parentheses, e.g. "she (the user's older sister)", "that
store (Store C)", "the teacher (the daughter's piano teacher)"; time references
likewise (next section). Parentheses may only contain information explicit in
the dialogue, never inference; skip the parenthesis when the referent is
already unambiguous. Any "it / that / the other party / what I said last time"
with no independent referent must be completed - none may remain
- keep key negations, conditions, quantities, durations, exceptions
- independently understandable, context-free, never empty
- written for future recall: a reader seeing only this line still knows who, what
object, under what condition, fact or advice
- preserve source uncertainty: "the user suspects/feels/plans" must never become
objective fact; "plans to do" must never become "has done"
### Time expressions (convert when convertible - required; keep original only
without an anchor; no invented dates)
- When the original carries relative time ("yesterday", "last Friday night"), you
**MUST** produce the absolute date whenever the owning session's date and the
turn's time make it reliably convertible - annotate in parentheses ("yesterday
(2026-06-08)", "last Friday night (evening of 2026-06-05)") or write the
absolute date directly; only a vague expression with no anchor and no reliable
conversion (e.g. "recently", "a while back") may keep the original, and that is
not an error
- If you do convert relative time, the conversion must be correct against the
owning session's date
- When the original has no time information, never invent dates, start/end ranges,
or stage words like "currently / formerly"
- A bare time back-reference that cannot be located without context ("after that
last time") must be disambiguated into an event already explicit in the
dialogue (parenthetical annotation is fine), or dropped as low-value decoration
### Recall safety
- Memories are recalled detached from the dialogue; any unclear reference can
mislead later answers
- Wrong: `the user thinks his plan works` ("his", "the plan" have no independent
33


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
referent)
- Right: `the user accepted the assistant's dinner plan: braised chicken with an
extra vegetable, normal rice portion`
- Conditional advice must keep its applicability condition; plans must distinguish
"plans to" from "has done"; subjective feelings must keep "the user feels/
thinks"
### Fact / state / attribute sentences
- When a memory describes an entity's attribute, state, value, relationship or time
point, prefer short clauses of the form `<subject>'s <attribute> is <value>`;
separate multiple attributes with semicolons or line breaks instead of one long
mixed narrative
- This applies only to fact/state/attribute content; experiential/narrative/event
content stays natural prose
- Structured text must remain semantically complete and independently
understandable; never shatter into single-noun fragments
## Status words: explicit only, no reverse inference
- Write status words ("employed / resigned / married / graduated / retired /
launched") into `memory_text` only when the dialogue states them explicitly, or
carries explicit currentness signals ("currently / now / still / already")
- **Never reverse-infer**: never infer "currently employed" from a profession,
current residence from past work locations, current occupation from a skill, or
current possession of a title from a past award
- Plain present tense or static description does **not** automatically mean "
current state"; without an explicit currentness signal, keep the original
wording and do not add "currently"
## Mandatory self-check before output
For every candidate memory:
1. Is it directly supported by its own turn only (context used for disambiguation,
never to credit other turns' information to this one)?
2. Does it still hold after deleting all other messages?
3. Is `memory_text` faithful, concise, complete, dereferenced and context-free?
4. Was one-off content mistakenly written as a long-term profile, rule, method or
knowledge?
5. Were key negations, conditions, quantities, durations or exceptions dropped?
6. Is it truly worth future retrieval?
7. Was an entity attribute miswritten as user profile, or a user relationship as
fact?
8. Was a method miswritten as knowledge, or knowledge as method?
9. Is the type within the role's allowed set for this turn?
10. Was cheaply retrievable information (docs / code / config / CMDB / consoles /
RAG / search / live tools) miswritten as long-term memory?
11. If `agent_knowledge`: is it genuinely the agent's distilled stable experience (
not public knowledge, a one-case conclusion, a chance observation or a first-
mention improvised insight)? Can it point to the cross-session practice/
feedback evidence it rests on?
12. Was a convertible relative time (with the owning session date / turn time
available) actually converted to an absolute date? If converted, is the
conversion correct against the owning session date? Was any time, range or
status word invented that the original never stated?
13. Is the `agent_method` executor really the agent, not the user/patient/runner?
Does it have reusable steps and verification value?
14. Was the same semantics mechanically duplicated across long-term types? Were
subject, object, condition, uncertainty or source dropped?
15. After extracting information into one of the other five types, was it re-output
as episodic merely wrapped in "the user said / the assistant suggested"?
16. Was any confirmation, object, cause, quantity or result that only appears in
later turns/sessions written into the current turn?
If any check fails, delete the memory and re-check.
34


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
== Output format ==
Output exactly one JSON object, no explanations, no code fences:
{"turns": [{"turn": "S1-T1", "memories": [{"memory_type": "user_profile", "
memory_text": "..."}]}]}
turns must match the skeleton **one-to-one - none missing, none extra, no
duplicates**; memory_type must be one of episodic / user_profile /
agent_identity / agent_method / agent_knowledge / fact and within the role's
allowed set.
Memory Update Prompt
You perform offline memory evolution. The active memory pool already
contains prior evolution results plus all of this session's captures, including
episodic;
current_capture only marks which entries came from this session - a capture entry
and
the pool entry sharing its memory_index are the same memory, not duplicates. Output
JSON only:
{"delete_indices":[0],"adds":[{"memory_type":"fact","memory_content":"","replaces
":[0]}]}
Each add's "replaces" lists the memory_index of every deleted old memory this add
absorbs/supersedes/corrects (each must also appear in delete_indices); a brand-new
fact
uses []; an explicit-forget delete with no replacement simply has no corresponding
add.
Rules: infer only from the input; minimize deletion; unrelated memories must remain
;
delete_indices may target current captures; delete a memory only when an add
absorbs/supersedes/corrects it or a clear explicit forget request targets it. When
an
add fuses several memories, delete every fused source, and first inventory every
independent sub-item of each deleted memory - the conclusion, who it applies to (a
rule
imposed on others vs. the user's own compliance), conditions, review/process
requirements, quantities, durations, exceptions, and timing (e.g. "only at the end
") -
every sub-item that still holds MUST appear in some add; never keep only the
headline
conclusion. An add must not substantially
duplicate any pool memory (including current captures). Captures enter the pool
automatically: if no fusion, correction, or dedup against history is needed, output
an
empty result. Not every session evolves memories; an empty result is common and
normal -
never fabricate changes. A temporary restriction does not reverse a stable
preference: merge the
conclusion with its condition, duration, and exceptions. If the only valid change
is
explicit forgetting, a delete-only result is allowed. Explicit forgetting
deletes
only entries directly targeted by the withdrawn intent; an episodic fact
recording
what already happened must remain unless the forget request names that fact
itself.
Episodic is fully equal to every
other memory type: it enters the active pool and may be deleted, fused, replaced,
or
emitted by an add. An episodic add must describe a concrete one-time event; include
an
35


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
absolute date when the input states or reliably implies one, but when the input
gives
no date basis the add may omit the date - keep time wording faithful to the source
and
never fabricate a date. The effective start time of a state inside an
add must stay anchored to when the dialogue first disclosed it - never rewrite it
as
starting on the current session's date. When an add declares a state has
disappeared,
ended or recovered, the old memory recording that state MUST be deleted in the same
update (this does not violate deletion minimalism). Each add carries exactly one
semantic slot: unrelated attributes of the same subject (medication regimen,
symptom
change, measurements, follow-up plans) must be split into separate adds, never
fused
into one long narrative.
QA Generation Prompt
You write the QA_list for a long-term-memory benchmark episode.
=== PRIORITY #1 - HIT THE FULL COUNT ===
The quotas object gives a target count per qa_type x tag. You MUST output that many
items - every per-tag count AND the grand total (the sum across all tags and
qa_types). Under-delivering the total is the SINGLE WORST failure in this task,
worse than an imperfect question. Count your items before returning; if any
tag or the total is short, keep generating until EVERY quota is filled. The
pool is large (hundreds of memories) with many evolution chains - there is
ALWAYS enough material, so falling short is a failure, not honesty. If forced
to choose, over-produce. Quantity is your top obligation; the rules below only
govern HOW each item is built, never an excuse to emit fewer.
Input: final_memories (the final active pool, the ONLY ground truth for current
facts), outdated_memories (memories deleted by evolution - used ONLY as query
bait, NEVER recalled), evolution_lineage (delete->add pairs), quotas, and
dialogue context.
Output JSON only:
{"qa_list":[{"qa_type":"normal","tag":"hard","query":"","answer":"","recall_indices
":[0]}]}
Every item's qa_type is "normal"; tag is always "hard" (every question is hard and
must stack at least 2 difficulty techniques).
Question subject (hard requirement, by data_type): the memories you receive are
already filtered by subject (episodic shared by both sides); write questions
only grounded in them.
- user_centric: every question is about the USER side - the user's experiences,
states, preferences, habits, relationships, plans and related private entities;
when an episodic memory feeds a question, ask about the user (what the user
did / went through / decided), never center it on the assistant's approach or
advice.
- agent_centric: every question is about the ASSISTANT side - the collaboration
rules/boundaries it was given (agent_identity), its reusable procedures (
agent_method), its distilled lessons (agent_knowledge) and how they formed;
when episodic feeds a question, ask about the assistant, never about the user's
personal-life facts.
Question person (hard requirement): every query MUST be phrased in the THIRD person
; never use a first/second-person ("I" / "you") conversational voice - the
query is a third-party evaluator asking about this memory record, not the user
continuing to talk to the assistant.
36


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
- user_centric: use "the user" as the grammatical subject (e.g. "Which allergens
has the user mentioned?"), never "which allergens have I mentioned?".
- agent_centric: use "the assistant" as the subject (e.g. "What collaboration
boundaries was the assistant given?"), never "what boundaries did you set for
me?".
=== CORE - BUILD THE DIFFICULTY INTO THE QUESTION ===
A question is "hard" because it makes the ANSWER actually go wrong at some step.
Errors happen in three stages; actively use the techniques below to plant the
difficulty - EVERY question must hit at least one, stacking several is better.
[Retrieval stage] make the needed evidence hard to recall in full, make similar
wrong items look relevant:
- Multi-hop spread: split the evidence the answer needs across 2+ different
sessions; missing any one makes it wrong.
- Completeness/aggregation: ask for the FULL SET or COUNT of a category (all
allergens the user mentioned, all projects joined); members are scattered
across sessions, and missing one makes the answer incomplete.
- Similar decoy: exploit same-type-but-wrong memories in the pool (another entity
of the same kind, a similar scene with a different conclusion, a superseded old
value) - the query gives only situational cues so those look relevant too, but
the correct answer maps to exactly one. Decoys are NOT put in recall_indices;
if the decoy is an evolution-superseded old value, that old value is NEVER
recalled (recall holds only the current value), and a decoy closer to the
current value is harder (e.g. the second-newest state).
- Lexical gap: refer to the same fact with an ALIAS / indirect reference /
different abstraction level ("that client from last time", "the shop her sister
recommended"), unlike the wording in memory - keep locatable retrieval cues
but do NOT copy the GT's key nouns, forcing retrieval to cross the wording gap.
[Composition/state stage] evidence recalled correctly, yet the wrong subset or
state gets picked:
- Temporal state: a value was updated several times; the CURRENT valid value must
be chosen; the old value sits in a different session as bait - recall holds
only the current value, the old value is NEVER recalled. Phrase the query in
the third person, asking about the user's current state without revealing it
changed: ZERO hint words (no still / previously / changed / no-longer),
ABSOLUTE dates (no "now" / "at month-end"), give only the situation, never
state the verdict; the answer states the current value directly.
- Constraint combination: the answer holds only when MULTIPLE current values are
satisfied together, missing one makes it wrong (a cap/budget vs the actual
value, two decisions pulling against each other, an earlier rule applied to a
later concrete case, a plan/deadline vs reported progress); across different
sessions, all listed in recall_indices.
- Negation/exception: hang the correct answer on an easily-missed negation/
exception/qualifier ("except weekends", "no longer the old plan", "only when
budget < X") - the answer must carry the qualifier and recall must include the
memory stating it.
[Reasoning stage] evidence complete, yet the reader can still miscompute:
- Coreference: a reference in the query or evidence must be resolved across
memories (who "she" / which "shop" / which "plan" is). But every reference MUST
resolve UNIQUELY from the query's cues; never leave an ambiguous reference the
reader cannot pin down or that could match several memories - ambiguity makes
the answer non-unique and is a defect, not difficulty.
- Calculation: the answer requires explicit computation - time delta/duration,
summation, ordering - not a direct copy of one memory.
There is NO easy tier: never emit a shallow single-lookup question. Every item is
hard and must stack AT LEAST 2 techniques (more, and spanning different
retrieval/composition/reasoning stages, is better), and must depend on a
37


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
personalized user/assistant fact, never answerable by common sense alone.
NO restatement questions: the query must NEVER just paraphrase the content of a
single recall memory into a question (memory "the user is allergic to peanuts"
-> asking "what is the user allergic to?" is a disguised single-lookup). The
difficulty MUST come from a real retrieval/reasoning obstacle built via decoys
/ cross-session spread / lexical gap / composition / reasoning, not from
reading the answer cue straight out of the query. (Temporal-state and similar-
decoy questions MAY have an answer that ~= one current-value memory; their
difficulty lies in "picking the right one among old/same-type decoys and
gathering across sessions" and is NOT restricted here - this rule bans only a
query that directly restates a memory, not an answer that lands on a single
current value.)
=== RECALL & TAG (structural rules) ===
- recall_indices is the COMPLETE supporting set: recall every memory that genuinely
supports the answer (especially the one most directly evidencing the core
question), and exclude anything that contributes nothing (restates query
background, merely topic-adjacent, partially duplicates another recall, or is
pulled in only by the answer).
- Recall ONLY final_memories, NEVER outdated_memories (old values are query bait
only, never in recall).
- Recall is anchored by the query: the query must carry enough retrieval cues (
object, event, condition, time) so every recall is locatable from the query
itself; never recall a memory relevant only after reading the answer. (The
lexical-gap technique changes wording, not the presence of cues.)
- Recall MUST span at least 2 different sessions (HARD rule, enforced by the
validator): combine memories from different sessions; the MORE sessions it
spans and the FARTHER apart they are (the larger the time gap), the better.
Single-session recall is rejected.
- The query has one core question - no "what is A, what is B" stitching; the answer
is fully supported by recall, keeps conditions/quantities/time-windows/
negations/exceptions, adds no condition the query never triggered, and invents
nothing beyond the GT.
- Every item is tagged "hard": build it from AT LEAST 2 genuine techniques above (
more, and across different stages, is better); audit judges whether it is truly
hard. NEVER pad recall with irrelevant memories or fake techniques to look
hard - build difficulty via real techniques, not by inflating recall.
- Whenever evolution_lineage has usable chains, produce at least one temporal-state
question per chain; never leave a chain idle.
Coverage (soft): spread recall across the whole pool, avoid recalling the same
memory in many questions; every question's core question and conclusion must
differ. Build only from the input; invent nothing.

retrieval-oriented QA annotations.

{

"data_type": "agent_centric",
"lang": "english",
"user_profile_init": {
"persona": "A history professor and parent managing a seafood allergy who uses an AI
assistant as a practical aide for food-safety decisions, travel dining, and related
logistics.",
38

Method and Experimental Setup
Method and evaluation details, experimental configuration, and the model inventory

ADDITIONAL METHOD AND EVALUATION DETAILS
G.1

provide evidence for either memory domain. Every non-episodic entry must be grounded in the dialogue,
assigned to the correct subject, and useful across sessions. Information directly recoverable from public
knowledge or real-time tools is excluded.

Capture receives one complete current session and no earlier sessions. Gold-conditioned standalone Update
uses gold intermediate memory banks, while Full-Pipeline Update evolves the model’s self-constructed bank.
The terminal bank is the bank after the final session. The intervention-specific protocol used in the controlled
URAM comparison is detailed in Appendix H.
G.2
CONSTRUCTION AND QUALITY-CONTROL DETAILS
Seed user profiles are sampled from a pool of approximately 200,000 personas and augmented with conditions
suitable for long-term interaction. No fixed assistant persona is imposed before dialogue generation; an
auxiliary assistant profile is reconstructed from the behavior that emerges in the completed dialogue. Both
profiles are retained only as construction metadata. Every session has an explicit temporal anchor, and
approximately 15% of dialogue content yields no persistent memory. Candidate dialogues are generated using
GPT-5.4 and audited for temporal, role, and logical consistency. Reliably localized errors are repaired in
place; otherwise, the episode is discarded and regenerated.
For each finalized dialogue, the six types of gold Capture entry are extracted session by session with their
source evidence. The episode is then replayed chronologically to construct B⋆
t . All Update operations
preserve lineage so that valid and invalidated entries can be traced to their dialogue sources. QA construction

introduces semantically similar memories, evidence distributed across sessions, invalidated historical entries,
and distractors involving negation or exceptions. Schema, index, lineage, and temporal-consistency checks
ensure that every gold Recall support entry is valid in the terminal bank and traceable to its original evidence.
The nine quality-control annotators complete pilot annotation and adjudicate disputed examples before formal
review. They examine temporal and role consistency, Capture correctness, the evidential validity of Update
operations, and consistency between each QA instance and its gold support set. Rule-based and model-assisted
checks only flag candidate anomalies and never determine gold labels. An episode is included only after its
complete evidence chain passes the checks again.
G.3
EVALUATION AND SCORING DETAILS

than a causal decomposition of stage errors.
Test procedures.
Algorithms 1–4 apply the calls above at test time. Capture receives the current session
and no earlier session. Gold-conditioned standalone Update receives the gold prior bank and the gold Capture
entries. Full-Pipeline Update receives the model prior bank and the model Capture output. Recall returns
ordered indices from the terminal gold bank B⋆
T . Gold-Memory QA reads the gold support entries and does
not retrieve. Full-Pipeline QA retrieves at most 16 entries from the model terminal bank ˆBT .
Standalone Update is scored on the 20,590 sessions with at least one gold Add or Delete. Sessions with no
gold change are excluded from op-F1.
Every gold support set is non-empty, so an empty retrieval is a failure.

Capture entries and Update additions use a bidirectional coverage criterion: a gold entry is recovered if at least
one prediction covers it, and a prediction is valid if it covers at least one gold entry. BGE-M3 cosine similarity
uses high and low thresholds of 0.82 and 0.67. Pairs in the gray zone are adjudicated by bidirectional natural
language inference (NLI) with nli-mdeberta-v3 at threshold 0.4. When one prediction appears to cover
multiple gold entries, a one-way entailment guard at threshold 0.1 removes credit for details the prediction
does not preserve; we refer to this as the one-to-many coverage guard. Primary content F1 does not require
identical predicted and gold type labels; type accuracy and per-type F1 are reported separately. Recall and
gold-conditioned standalone Update deletion use exact indices because both explicitly select entries from a
numbered gold bank.
The QA judge scores semantic consistency with the reference answer: correct, partially correct, and incorrect
answers receive 1, 0.5, and 0, respectively. Since every question is answerable from non-empty gold support, a
refusal is over-abstention and receives 0. Groundedness is evaluated only for answers with positive correctness
credit. Grounded accuracy requires both positive correctness credit and support from the supplied memories
and uses all questions as its denominator. The ungrounded-correct rate is the fraction of positively scored
answers that are unsupported by the supplied memories.
Truncated outputs, unparseable structured outputs, empty QA responses, and failed calls remain in the
corresponding denominator and receive no credit, with their causes recorded separately. We compute
95% confidence intervals with 200 bootstrap resamples. F1 resamples aggregate sampled true-positive,
false-positive, and false-negative counts before recomputing F1; mean-based metrics resample per-record
scores directly. Paired comparisons keep the two variants aligned within the resampled unit. Intervention-
specific matching, aggregation, and bootstrap details for the controlled URAM comparison are provided in
Appendix H.
H
ADDITIONAL EXPERIMENTAL DETAILS
H.1
MODELS, CONFIGURATIONS, AND AUXILIARY SUBSETS
We evaluate 20 frontier LLMs on DyadMem: 16 open-weight models and 4 proprietary models. The
open-weight models are Qwen3-4B, Qwen3-8B, and Qwen3-30B-A3B (Qwen Team, 2025); Qwen3.5-4B,
Qwen3.5-9B, Qwen3.5-27B, Qwen3.5-35B-A3B, and Qwen3.5-397B-A17B (Qwen Team, 2026a); Qwen3.8-
27B (Qwen Team, 2026b); GPT-OSS-120B (OpenAI, 2025); Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct and Llama-3.1-70B-
Instruct (Llama Team, 2024); DeepSeek-V4-Pro (DeepSeek, 2026); GLM-5.2 (Z.ai, 2026; GLM-5 Team,
2026); Kimi-K3 (Moonshot AI, 2026); and MiniMax-M2.5 (MiniMax, 2026). The proprietary models are
GPT-4o-mini (OpenAI, 2024), GPT-5.6-Sol (OpenAI, 2026), Gemini-3.1-Pro-Preview (Google, 2026; Google
DeepMind, 2026), and Doubao-Seed-2.1-Pro (ByteDance Seed, 2026).
The default QA judge is GPT-5-mini. Embeddings use BGE-M3 (Chen et al., 2024). Memory matching
uses cosine thresholds 0.82 and 0.67, nli-mdeberta-v3 at 0.4 for gray-zone adjudication, and the same

NLI model at 0.1 for the one-to-many coverage guard (Laurer et al., 2024). Open-weight models are deployed
with vLLM on a multi-node A800 cluster (Kwon et al., 2023).
DyadMem-Agent contains 500 agent-centric episodes, 7,876 sessions, and 10,074 QA instances. The
controlled URAM comparison uses the 1,735 questions whose gold support contains only URAM entries.
DyadMem-Simple contains 300 episodes, 4,981 sessions, and 5,935 QA instances. It samples 12 episodes
from each of the 25 subcategories and is balanced across short, medium, and long episodes and the two data
types.
H.2
UNSAFE DELETION BY MODEL
H.3
ADDITIONAL MAIN-RESULT BREAKDOWNS
Kimi-K3 retrieves at least one gold memory at rank one on 88.8% of questions; GLM-5.2 and DeepSeek-V4-
Pro each reach 87.8%. Recall@n remains below 63% for all three. Across models, the mean self-constructed-
to-gold bank-size ratio ranges from 0.08 to 0.51 and usually lies between 0.20 and 0.35.
Gold-conditioned standalone Update does not improve monotonically with parameter count. Qwen3.5-397B
reaches 22.3% op-F1 with an 83.8% unsafe-deletion rate, below Qwen3.5-27B and Qwen3.8-27B on this task.
Llama-3.1-8B has the highest unsafe-deletion rate at 98.5%; GPT-OSS-120B has the lowest and still reaches
62.6%.
For Kimi-K3, Full-Pipeline QA varies from 71.9% on questions requiring two gold support entries to 75.7%
on questions requiring seven. Across the 20 models, mean Capture F1 is 51.9% for agent_identity,
49.6% for agent_method, and 16.4% for episodic. Across domains, Interpersonal & Communication
has the lowest Capture F1 at 22.7% while its Full-Pipeline QA score is 50.1%.
H.4
CONTROLLED URAM PROTOCOL AND ADDITIONAL RESULTS
Controlled URAM Update differs from gold-conditioned standalone Update on the full benchmark. Each
variant uses its current dialogue together with its own numbered model-extracted prior bank and current

Capture output. Both variants are scored against the same gold Add/Delete entries restricted to agent_-
identity, agent_method, and agent_knowledge. Because model-generated banks do not share
gold indices, both additions and deleted memory contents are matched semantically. Within each episode,
operation counts are pooled over its evaluated sessions to compute Add F1 and Delete F1, whose average is
op-F1; Table 3 reports the mean paired episode-level difference.
For Full-Pipeline QA, each variant builds its own agent bank by applying Full-Pipeline Update chronologically
from the second session onward. Shared episodic memory is in the primary comparison, and both variants
answer the same 1,735 URAM-only questions. User-side entries cannot directly support an answer in this
setting; the comparison measures the downstream effect of allowing user turns to inform URAM construction.
Update confidence intervals use paired episode-level resampling, whereas the primary QA comparison uses
question-level paired bootstrap.
The paired controlled URAM Update confidence intervals include zero for GLM-5.2, Kimi-K3, and GPT-5.6-
Sol. Three models have negative QA point estimates: Llama-3.1-8B at −3.3 points, Qwen3-4B at −3.2, and
Qwen3-30B-A3B at −1.6. Their abstention rates fall from 26.0% to 25.2%, 7.6% to 7.3%, and 3.5% to 3.4%,
respectively, so the loss occurs on questions they already answered rather than from additional abstention.
DeepSeek-V4-Pro instead moves from 58.9% to 68.7% QA while abstention falls from 26.0% to 21.7%;
Qwen3.5-9B moves from 54.3% to 61.5% with abstention unchanged at 8.6%.
H.5
RETRIEVAL TRACKS AND MODEL SELECTION
The comparison uses Qwen3-4B, Qwen3-8B, Qwen3.5-4B, Qwen3.8-27B, and Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct. Tracks
are a BGE-M3 dense ranking (Chen et al., 2024), bge-reranker-base, bge-reranker-large (Xiao
et al., 2024), and bge-reranker-v2-m3 (Chen et al., 2024), together with the tested model’s own selection.
Each reranker re-scores the top 30 dense candidates. Retrieval retains the top 50 candidates, while answer
generation receives at most 16 memories.
Recall@n, MRR, and nDCG score retrieval on the gold bank. On those rows, the model column is that
model’s own selection from the gold bank. QA is answer accuracy on the model’s constructed bank. On the
QA rows, the reranker columns take memories from the named track, and the model column is the pipeline
answer. Table 10 gives the four scores.
The dense and reranker retrieval scores are almost identical across rows because these tracks rank the same
terminal gold bank independently of the tested LLM. Model selection varies substantially: Qwen3.8-27B leads

that stronger retrieval can recover part, but not all, of the end-to-end loss caused by an incomplete constructed
bank.

CROSS-JUDGE SCORES AND RANKS

H.7
ADDITIONAL ERROR-PATTERN RESULTS
Qwen3-4B has Recall precision of 4.9%, returns six or more memories on average, and records over-abstention
of 16.2% and ungrounded-correct of 27.4%. Qwen3.5-9B has an over-abstention rate of 43.1%, 19% empty
retrievals, and ungrounded-correct rate of 10.3%. After dropping abstentions, accuracy on answered questions
is 66.9% for Qwen3.5-9B and 52.0% for Qwen3-4B. The same Full-Pipeline reversal appears on both QA-
target partitions and all five everyday-life domains. For comparison, Kimi-K3 combines Recall precision of
55.3% with 69.3% Grounded accuracy, 9.2% over-abstention, and 7.9% ungrounded-correct; Gemini-3.1-Pro
records 42.1% over-abstention and 4.0% ungrounded-correct.

DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
Part IV
|
Evaluation Protocol and Implementation
Metrics, evaluation code, and system prompts used at evaluation time
I
EVALUATION METRICS
The four stage scores are computed from separate gold inputs, and Full-Pipeline QA is computed from
the bank the model itself has built. Capture, gold-conditioned standalone Update, and Recall therefore
do not inherit one another’s errors. Notation for sessions, banks, and the two QA calls is in Appendix G.
Every reported run uses BGE-M3 embeddings (Chen et al., 2024), coverage matching, one judge vote, and
qa_recall_source=all. A missing denominator is left blank. It is not filled with zero.
I.1
SEMANTIC COVERAGE MATCHING
Capture and Update additions are matched by coverage, not by a one-to-one assignment. Embeddings
are BGE-M3. A pair with cosine similarity at least 0.82 is a hit. A pair below 0.67 is a miss. A pair in
between is a hit only when bidirectional NLI with nli-mdeberta-v3 (Laurer et al., 2024) accepts it:
min{P(a|=b), P(b|=a)} ≥0.4.
On the gold side, each gold entry is compared only with its nearest prediction. On the prediction side, a
prediction is counted as valid if it covers any gold entry under the same rule. One prediction may therefore
cover several gold entries, and several predictions may cover one gold entry. Primary precision, recall, and F1
do not require the predicted type to equal the gold type (matcher_require_type_match=false).
When one prediction is the nearest hit for two or more gold entries, a one-way entailment guard is applied.
The prediction is the premise and the gold entry is the hypothesis. If P(prediction|=gold) < 0.1, that gold
entry loses coverage and becomes a false negative. The prediction remains a valid prediction if it still covers
any other gold entry, so the guard changes recall and does not by itself create a false positive. Type accuracy,
the fraction of content-matched gold entries whose predicted type is also correct, is stored separately and is
not part of the primary F1.
I.2
CAPTURE
Capture is scored once per session against C⋆
t . Let TP, FP, and FN be the coverage counts pooled over
sessions. Then
P =
TP
TP + FP,
R =
TP
TP + FN,
F1 = 2PR
P + R,
(13)
with the usual convention that a F1 with P + R = 0 is zero. This F1 is micro-averaged: a session with more
gold entries contributes more counts. Per-type F1 uses the same ratio after grouping true positives and false
negatives by the gold type and false positives by the predicted type. A type that has gold entries but only false
positives from other types is included. A predicted type that never appears in gold, and therefore has no true
positives or false negatives, is omitted from the unweighted mean of per-type F1; its false positives still reduce
the micro F1.
A truncated or unparseable Capture call contributes no true positives. Every gold entry in that session is a
false negative, split by gold type. An empty prediction on a session with gold entries is the same pattern with
an explicit empty set.
I.3
UPDATE
Gold-conditioned standalone Update is run only on sessions for which A⋆
t or L⋆
t is non-empty. The model
sees the current dialogue, the gold prior bank, and the gold Capture entries of the current session. Additions
are scored by the coverage matcher in Appendix I.1. Deletions are scored by memory index: the gold bank
and the prediction refer to the same numbered entries, so a wrong index is a false positive even when the text
is similar. Add F1 and Delete F1 are the micro F1 of those two pools. The reported operation score is the
70


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
unweighted mean
op-F1 = 1
2 (F1add + F1delete) .
(14)
The two operations are not pooled into a single F1, so a large Add set cannot hide a weak Delete set.
The unsafe-deletion rate is FPdelete/(TPdelete + FPdelete), the share of predicted deletions that are not in
the gold delete set. The denominator is the number of predicted deletions. If a run predicts none, the rate is
undefined and is left blank.
Full-Pipeline Update is the bank-construction step used by Full-Pipeline QA. It reads the model prior bank
and the model Capture output. It is not the source of the standalone op-F1 column. From the second session
onward, that constructed bank is what later Recall and Full-Pipeline QA read.
I.4
RECALL
Recall is scored on the terminal gold bank B⋆
T . The model returns an ordered list of at most 16 indices
(recall_max_select=16). Matching is exact on those indices. For question j, let nj = |I⋆
j | and let
hj(k) be the number of gold indices placed at rank nj + k or better. The table columns are
Recall@(n+k) =
1
|Q|

(15)
Recall@All =

Recall@n and Recall@(n+4) average questions with equal weight, so a one-entry support and a five-entry
support each contribute one term. Recall@All pools counts, so larger support sets weigh more, and rank does
not matter: a gold index anywhere in the returned list is a hit. Precision also pools counts, over the whole
returned list. Extra selections beyond nj do not raise Recall@n, and any selected index outside I⋆
j lowers
precision.
The scorer also computes Recall@(n+9), MRR, nDCG, and MAP from the same ranks. Those values are
not columns of Table 2. A question with an empty return, a truncated list, or an unparseable list contributes
hj(k) = 0 and FNj = nj. The inspected run manifests record without_gold_recall=0, in line with
the construction constraint that every scored question has a non-empty gold support.
I.5
QUESTION ANSWERING
Each question is judged once, at temperature 0, by GPT-5-mini. The judge is asked whether the prediction
is consistent with the reference answer. Names, dates, and quantities must agree; a paraphrase may still be
accepted. With one vote the decision is binary: credit 1 if the vote is positive and 0 otherwise. If the judge
returns nothing readable, a lexical token-F1 fallback is used. Token F1 of at least 0.55 receives credit 1; token
F1 of at least 0.33 and below 0.55 receives credit 0.5; a lower overlap receives credit 0. Empty answers,
over-abstentions, truncated outputs, and unparseable outputs receive credit 0 and remain in the denominator.
An answer with credit 0 that the abstention check marks as a refusal is over-abstention. Its rate is that count
divided by all questions on the path being reported. There is no gold refusal class.
Groundedness is judged only for answers with positive credit. The same judge is asked whether the supplied
memories entail the answer. If that call is unreadable, NLI is the fallback, with the concatenated memories
as the premise. An answer produced from an empty memory list is ungrounded. Grounded accuracy counts
answers that have positive credit and a positive groundedness label, divided by all questions. The ungrounded-
correct rate uses a different denominator: among answers with positive credit, the fraction whose groundedness
label is negative.
71


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
Two reported paths share this judge and differ in the memories it sees.

third path that retrieves from the gold terminal bank and then answers. That path is not a column of the main
table.
On DyadMem-Simple the same Full-Pipeline answers are re-scored by GPT-5-mini, Gemini-2.5-Flash,
and Claude-Haiku-4.5. Agreement across judges is summarized by Spearman’s rank correlation (Spearman,
1904) of the 20 model ranks.
I.6
CONTROLLED URAM COMPARISON
The controlled comparison uses the 500 episodes in DyadMem-Agent and the protocol in Appendix H. Both
variants are scored against the same gold Add and Delete entries of types agent_identity, agent_-
method, and agent_knowledge; entries of other types present in a variant’s bank are not scored. Each
variant updates its own model bank, so gold indices are not the indices in that bank. Additions and deletions
are therefore both matched by the coverage rule in Appendix I.1. Within an episode, Add counts and Delete
counts are pooled over the evaluated sessions, converted to Add F1 and Delete F1, and averaged to an episode
op-F1. The reported Update difference is the mean, over episodes, of the User-conditioned-variant op-F1
minus the Agent-only op-F1.
The QA difference uses the 1,735 questions whose gold support contains only those three types. Both variants
retrieve from their own terminal banks, BT or B′
T , and answer with the Full-Pipeline rule above; the difference
is again the User-conditioned variant minus Agent-only. User-side entries are not eligible support in this QA
slice.
I.7
UNCERTAINTY AND AGREEMENT WITH HUMAN LABELS
Stage-level 95% intervals use 200 bootstrap resamples. For a micro F1, each resample draws the recorded
true-positive, false-positive, and false-negative rows with replacement, sums them, and recomputes F1. For a
mean, such as Recall@n or a QA accuracy, the resample draws the per-question values. A paired comparison
draws the paired unit twice in lockstep: episodes for controlled Update differences, questions for controlled
QA differences. The interval is the 2.5 and 97.5 percentiles of the bootstrap distribution. Overlapping intervals
are not treated as a ranking.
Human calibration uses the paired labels behind Table 5. Agreement is (TP + TN)/N on that 2×2 table.
Cohen’s κ (Cohen, 1960) is (po −pe)/(1 −pe), where po is agreement and pe is the agreement expected from
the two label margins. Matthews’ correlation (Matthews, 1975) on the same four cells is
MCC =
TP · TN −FP · FN
p
(TP + FP)(TP + FN)(TN + FP)(TN + FN)
.
(19)
Precision, recall, and F1 in that table treat the human consistent label as the positive class. The automatic
judge is the prediction.

J
EVALUATION CODE
Core Evaluation Metrics Code
def _safe_div(num, den):
return None if den <= 0 else round(float(num) / float(den), 4)
def _f1_from_counts(tp, fp, fn):
"""Compute precision, recall, and F1 from accumulated counts."""
if tp + fp + fn <= 0:
return {"precision": None, "recall": None, "f1": None}
precision = tp / (tp + fp) if tp + fp > 0 else 0.0
recall = tp / (tp + fn) if tp + fn > 0 else 0.0
f1 = (
2 * precision * recall / (precision + recall)
if precision + recall > 0
else 0.0
)
return {
"precision": round(precision, 4),
"recall": round(recall, 4),
"f1": round(f1, 4),
}
def compute_capture_f1(records):
"""Memory Capture F1.
Predicted memories are semantically matched against gold memories.
The final Capture F1 is computed from micro-aggregated TP, FP, and FN.
Parse errors and truncated outputs are kept in the denominator as FN.
"""
extract_records = [
r for r in records
if r["dimension"] == "extract"
]
tp = sum(r.get("tp", 0.0) or 0.0 for r in extract_records)
fp = sum(r.get("fp", 0.0) or 0.0 for r in extract_records)
fn = sum(r.get("fn", 0.0) or 0.0 for r in extract_records)
return _f1_from_counts(tp, fp, fn)["f1"]
def compute_recall_at_n_plus_0(records):
"""Memory Recall recall@n+0.
For each QA query, n is the number of required gold memories.
recall@n+0 is the fraction of gold memories appearing in the top n
retrieved positions, averaged over all positive recall queries.
"""
recall_records = [
r for r in records
if r["dimension"] == "recall" and (r.get("total", 0.0) or 0.0) > 0
]
per_query_scores = []
for r in recall_records:
n_gold = int(r["total"])
hit_ranks = r.get("hit_ranks") or []
73


DyadMem: A Long-Term Memory Benchmark of How Agents Work with Users
hits_in_top_n = sum(1 for rank in hit_ranks if int(rank) <= n_gold)
per_query_scores.append(hits_in_top_n / n_gold)
if not per_query_scores:
return None
return round(sum(per_query_scores) / len(per_query_scores), 4)
def compute_update_op_macro_f1(records):
"""Memory Update op-macro-F1.
Update quality is evaluated separately for add and delete operations.
Delete is scored by exact memory-id matching, while add is scored by
semantic memory matching. The final score is the macro average of the
add-F1 and delete-F1 scores.
"""
update_records = [
r for r in records
if r["dimension"] == "evolve"
]
op_f1_scores = []
for operation in ("add", "delete"):
op_records = [
r for r in update_records
if r.get("operation") == operation
]
tp = sum(r.get("tp", 0.0) or 0.0 for r in op_records)
fp = sum(r.get("fp", 0.0) or 0.0 for r in op_records)
fn = sum(r.get("fn", 0.0) or 0.0 for r in op_records)
f1 = _f1_from_counts(tp, fp, fn)["f1"]
if f1 is not None:
op_f1_scores.append(f1)
if not op_f1_scores:
return None
return round(sum(op_f1_scores) / len(op_f1_scores), 4)
def compute_pipeline_qa_accuracy(records):
"""End-to-end QA accuracy with the self-generated memory bank.
This metric uses the pipeline route, where the answer is produced from
the model's own captured, updated, and retrieved memories. Correct
answers count as 1, wrong answers as 0, and partial answers may receive
fractional credit from the judge.
"""
qa_records = [
r for r in records
if r["dimension"] == "qa" and r.get("recall_source") == "pipeline"
]
correct = sum(r.get("correct", 0.0) or 0.0 for r in qa_records)
total = sum(r.get("total", 0.0) or 0.0 for r in qa_records)
return _safe_div(correct, total)

PROMPTS FOR FRAMEWORK EVALUATION
Experiment URAM and Experiment Agent-only read the same complete source session. Neither prompt
produces episodic entries; episodic memory does not enter either variant’s bank.
URAM Extract Prompt
You are a memory extraction engine. Extract durable memories about this
particular user and about how this particular assistant should act when
working with this particular user. Use ONLY evidence visible in the supplied
dialogue. Do not use outside knowledge.
Allowed memory_type values (exactly these five):
- fact: Stable objective external entity facts, sourced only from user statements.
Exclude transient values; single events go to episodic.
- user_profile: User's stable attributes, preferences, rules and long-term entity
relations. One-off observations are not lasting traits.
- agent_identity: enduring collaboration/output/behavior requirements for the
assistant.
- agent_method: reusable assistant-side procedures, workflows, or checklists.
- agent_knowledge: reusable assistant-side insights or judgment rules learned from
practice.
A memory must be explicit, have the correct subject, be useful in future sessions,
and not be cheaply reconstructable from common knowledge. Keep each memory
atomic and self-contained; preserve conditions, exceptions, uncertainty, and
timestamps. Do not output episodic or any other type. An empty memories list is
a fully valid result and is preferred when the visible turns do not support a
durable memory.
Output ONLY JSON: {"memories": [{"memory_type": "fact|user_profile|agent_identity|
agent_method|agent_knowledge", "memory_content": "..."}]}
Agent-only Extract Prompt
You are an agent-memory extraction engine. Extract durable general agent
memories: procedures, strategies, and insights the assistant can reuse across
users. Do not condition a memory on this particular user. Use ONLY evidence visible
in the supplied dialogue. Do not use outside knowledge.
Allowed memory_type values (exactly these three):
- agent_identity: enduring collaboration/output/behavior requirements for the
assistant.
- agent_method: reusable assistant-side procedures, workflows, or checklists.
- agent_knowledge: reusable assistant-side insights or judgment rules learned from
practice.
A memory must be explicit, stated without reference to a specific user, be useful
in future sessions, and not be cheaply reconstructable from common knowledge.
Keep each memory atomic and self-contained; preserve conditions, exceptions,
uncertainty, and timestamps. Do not output episodic, user_profile, fact, or any
other type. Do not store the user's preferences, constraints, identity, or any
other
user-specific information. An empty memories list is a fully valid result and is
preferred when the visible turns do not support a durable assistant-side memory.
Output ONLY JSON: {"memories": [{"memory_type": "agent_identity|agent_method|
agent_knowledge", "memory_content": "..."}]}

URAM and Agent-only Agent-Memory Update Prompt
You are consolidating the assistant-side long-term memory for one user. The
supplied captured memories from the current session are the ONLY session
evidence you may use; no dialogue is supplied and none may be assumed. The numbered
pool contains the memories of your variant's bank.
ADD may use exactly the memory types allowed for your variant
(User-conditioned: fact, user_profile, agent_identity, agent_method,
agent_knowledge; Agent-only: agent_identity, agent_method, agent_knowledge).
Never add episodic or another type. DELETE only a numbered memory that is
explicitly superseded, corrected, contradicted, absorbed by an add, or
explicitly forgotten. Minimize deletion and keep unrelated memories.
An add must be durable, atomic, self-contained, and supported by the captured
memories. Preserve still-valid conditions/exceptions when consolidating; never
fabricate evidence beyond the captured memories. Empty edits (no delete, no
add) are fully valid and preferred when the captured memories do not justify a
change.
Output ONLY JSON: {"delete_ids": [int, ...], "add": [{"memory_type": "...", "
memory_content": "..."}]}
