QUALITATIVE MODEL RESULTS
This appendix presents ten representative error-case displays from the selected QA set. The cases cover
timeline confusion, adjacent rule leakage, incomplete or over-inclusive sets, example-to-rule bridging failure,
fine-grained entity substitution, and mixed errors. The selection improves domain coverage where possible
while keeping the overall data-type balance at five agent-centric and five user-centric examples.
L.1
TYPE I: TIMELINE CONFUSION
Case 1: Timeline Confusion
Category. Physical Health / Dietary Restrictions
Data type. agent centric
Query. How should the assistant frame social-eating advice in tone and objective when perfection
is unrealistic and the user is already out, using both the earlier family-meal framing and the later
out-of-home compression?
Reference Answer. The assistant should frame social-eating advice as damage prevention rather
than perfection-chasing, keep it compact and immediately actionable, and aim for a workable real
meal rather than a punishment plate.
Case Analysis
This timeline-confusion case requires combining the earlier family-meal framing with the later out-
of-home compression. Correct answers preserve all three current requirements: damage prevention
rather than perfection, compact and immediately actionable advice, and a workable real meal rather
than a punishment plate.
Model Responses.
Qwen3-4B — Correct
Answer. The assistant should frame social-eating advice in a tone that is ordinary, controlled, and
not joyless, emphasizing practicality and immediate action. The objective is to maintain a balanced
approach, using the earlier family-meal framing as a foundation for mindful eating and the later
out-of-home compression as a guide for concise, actionable decisions that align with the user’s
goals, even when perfection is unrealistic and the user is already out.
Reason. Correct: combines damage prevention, compact actionable advice, and the real-meal
objective.

L.2
TYPE II: ADJACENT RULE LEAKAGE
Case 2: Adjacent Rule Leakage
Category. Physical Health / Workout Planning
Data type. agent centric
Query. Under the assistant’s standard check-in format, which line should state whether the issue
is a preference problem, a constraint problem, or nothing at all, and what exact substitute should
appear when nothing is wrong?
Reference Answer. The fourth line should state the problem type, and when nothing is wrong the

Case 5: Incomplete or Over-Inclusive Set
Category. Career & Learning / Project Responsibilities
Data type. agent centric
Query. How should the assistant decide what belongs in leadership messaging when different
audiences need separate release notes, and what evidence suggested that this separation worked?
Reference Answer. The assistant should keep the audiences separate and include only decision-
relevant information for leadership, which was supported by the user’s report that the separate-
audience rule helped and by Dana’s very fast reply to the leadership version.
Case Analysis
This set-boundary case tests whether the model preserves the leadership-message boundary. Correct
answers keep audiences separate, restrict leadership notes to decision-relevant content, and cite
both the user report and Dana’s fast reply as evidence.
Model Responses.
Qwen3-4B — Incorrect
Answer. The assistant should decide what belongs in leadership messaging by focusing on decision,
impact, and ask for leadership updates, and scope, owner, and next step for team updates. Evidence
that this separation worked includes the user asking for both leadership and team release notes with
distinct wording, the assistant drafting separate release notes for leadership and team, and the user
noting that mixed-audience messages tend to sound mushy. Additionally, the user reported that the
audience-relative messaging principle helped avoid overexplaining.
Reason. Wrong: misses Dana’s fast reply.
Qwen3-8B — Incorrect
Answer. The assistant should decide what belongs in leadership messaging by focusing on decision,
impact, and ask, while keeping team messaging focused on scope, owner, and next step. The
evidence that this separation worked is that the assistant drafted separate Atlas 3.3 release notes
for leadership and for the team, both framed around the 14th release target and current-scope
assumptions, and the user indicated that mixed-audience messages tend to sound mushy.
Reason. Wrong: misses audience separation, Dana’s fast reply.
Qwen3-30B-A3B — Correct
Answer. The assistant should decide what belongs in leadership messaging by focusing on decision-
relevant risk, ensuring the main signal stays clear and the messages are short, audience-specific, and
honest about what is still conditional. The evidence that this separation worked includes the user’s
report that the separate-audience rule helped a lot when explaining the Atlas 3.4 status upward, and
the assistant’s observation that Dana’s very fast reply suggested the leadership update fit its audience
and was easy to act on.
Reason. Correct: keeps audiences separate, limits leadership to decision-relevant content, and cites
both success signals.

TYPE VI: MIXED ERROR
Case 10: Mixed Error
Category. Career & Learning / Collaboration Style
Data type. agent centric
Query. When a vendor proposes a quick sync, how should the assistant decide between email and a
call, and what broader meeting policy does that preserve?
Reference Answer. The assistant should test the purpose first: if the issue is only confirming facts,
open points, or a simple choice, push for email; if it requires live negotiation or site-dependent
decisions, support a call or walk. That preserves the rule that calls are for movement, not symbolism.
Case Analysis
This mixed-error case combines purpose testing, channel choice, and the broader meeting policy.
Correct answers choose email for factual confirmation or simple choices, reserve calls or walks for
live negotiation or site-dependent decisions, and preserve calls for genuine ambiguity.
Model Responses.
Qwen3-4B — Incorrect
Answer. When a vendor proposes a quick sync, the assistant should first test the purpose; if the
issue is only confirming facts, open points, or a simple choice, push for email; if it requires live
negotiation or site-dependent decisions, support a call or walk. This decision split preserves the
user’s broader meeting policy that calls are for movement rather than symbolism and prefers a
25-minute check-in every other Tuesday via email when there is no live deadline or decision.
Reason. Wrong: gives the channel rule without the preserving policy.
Qwen3-8B — Incorrect
Answer. When a vendor proposes a quick sync, the assistant should push for email if the purpose
is confirming facts, open points, or a simple choice, and support a call if live negotiation or site-
dependent decisions are needed. This decision preserves the user’s every-other-Tuesday meeting
policy, treating calls as for movement rather than symbolism.
Reason. Wrong: misses the broader meeting policy.

the issue is only confirming facts, open points, or a simple choice, push for email; if it requires live
negotiation or site-dependent decisions, support a call or walk. This decision preserves the user’s
existing meeting policy, which views calls as for movement rather than symbolism.
Reason. Correct: tests the purpose, routes simple facts to email, and reserves calls for live ambiguity.
